1	# Replicate (n = 1): the video
2	
3	The treatment is `../TREATMENT.md`: a music video typeset like the methods section of a paper,
4	every lyric line a figure, the baby the one coloured thing on the page. It is built on the
5	shared renderer (`shared/renderer/`, the song-code contract is described in
6	`songs/2am-crying/video/README.md`). Every frame is a pure function of song time.
7	
8	This directory is the **spine**. The page, the three permanent elements, every section plate
9	and the loop are done. Every verse figure is a placeholder: the right caption and number,
10	the legend, an empty framed figure area, and the baby in the cap standing in it, curious.
11	The second pass replaces the placeholders, one lab module at a time (see "The scene
12	interface" below).
13	
14	## Commands
15	
16	All from the repo root (`/Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop`). `--layout 16x9` is required:
17	render.mjs picks its default size from the flag, not from the song.
18	
19	```sh
20	node shared/renderer/scripts/serve.mjs     # preview:
21	# http://localhost:8123/songs/baby-facts/video/index.html?t=50
22	# http://localhost:8123/songs/baby-facts/video/index.html?plate=tronick      (a lab's hero frame)
23	# http://localhost:8123/songs/baby-facts/video/index.html?layout=9x16&t=12
24	# http://localhost:8123/songs/baby-facts/video/index.html?data=data-radio-sim/&t=100
25	
26	A=songs/baby-facts/audio/runA/replicate-n-1-6b228558.mp3
27	caffeinate -i node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/baby-facts/video --audio $A \
28	  --layout 16x9 --w 960 --h 540 --fps 30 --out songs/baby-facts/video/renders/draft-spine-540p.mp4
29	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/baby-facts/video --layout 16x9 --w 960 --h 540 --check
30	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/baby-facts/video --layout 16x9 --w 960 --h 540 \
31	  --stills 12,50,57,62 --stills-dir songs/baby-facts/video/renders/stills
32	node shared/renderer/scripts/render.mjs --song songs/baby-facts/video --layout 16x9 \
33	  --sheet songs/baby-facts/video/renders/plates.png --title plates
34	node shared/renderer/scripts/smoke.mjs --song songs/baby-facts/video --q t=3 --q "layout=9x16&t=57"
35	node songs/baby-facts/video/tools/figures.mjs [--data data-radio-sim]   # the edit: windows, figure numbers, captions
36	node songs/baby-facts/video/tools/radio_sim.mjs                          # rebuild data-radio-sim/
37	```
38	
39	The full 540p draft takes about 40 seconds with 9 workers on this Mac (the spine is light;
40	real figures will cost more). Run it under `caffeinate -i` (the laptop sleeps otherwise).
41	
42	## Data
43	
44	- `data/`: the keeper take, runA `6b228558` (269.6 s, 95.6 BPM), copied from
45	  `../audio/runA/data/`. Lines carry `section` (intro, verse1, chorus1, verse2, verse3, chorus2,
46	  verse4, bridge, break, drop, final, outro). `audio.json` names the mp3 in `source` (the
47	  preview plays it) and the sheet `suno_lyrics_v1_short.txt` in `lyrics_sheet`.
48	- `data-radio-sim/`: the same timing with the eight lines the radio cut
49	  (`suno_lyrics_v2_radio.txt`) drops removed (`tools/radio_sim.mjs`). It exists to test absent
50	  lines: `--data data-radio-sim --check`. It has no audio of its own.
51	- A new take only needs its `lyrics.json` and `audio.json` in `data/`. Figures, numbers and
52	  the loop are all derived from the lyric content.
53	
54	## The page and the permanent elements
55	
56	| element | file | notes |
57	|---|---|---|
58	| paper | `page.js` | `#f4efe3` stock, a 32 px (5 mm) `#e6dfcf` grid, per-pixel grain, faint mottling, darker edges; baked once per output size, blitted every frame |
59	| ink | `shared/renderer/core/ink.js` | the wobble pen: strokes are filled ribbons that wobble by noise of their arclength, seeded by plate + stroke key; `p` draws an exact prefix, so strokes grow without wiggling |
60	| HUD | `hud.js`, text from `story.js` | `n = 1`, mono in a hand-ruled box; the bridge shows each myth's n as it is named (`n = 4`, `n = 36 (students)`, `$105,000` for two beats on "Georgia", `n = 0 words`, `n = 6 (1977)`, `n = 106 (2016)`, `n = 567 · 37 labs`, `n = 42 · 1 h / month`), a fast shuffle of them on "So when you ask me", `n = ?` in the break, rolling digits in the drop that settle to `n = 1` on "replication" |
61	| caption | `caption.js` | the rule, then `Fig. k.` (italic), the title, the citation and note in the light ink: "Fig. 7. Growth saltation in one infant (Lampl, 1992)." Written in left to right at each figure start |
62	| legend | `legend.js` | the current line in the serif, left-aligned, two rows at most, wrapping at a clause, 40 px (34 min) at 1080p; unsung words light ink, sung words full ink with a wipe through the active word (core `karaoke.js wordProgress`); parentheticals italic and smaller. Hidden in the break |
63	
64	Fonts: `index.html` declares `Paper Serif` (Iowan Old Style, via `local()`) and `Paper Mono`
65	(SF Mono if installed system-wide, else Menlo) so the boot can verify they loaded. SF Mono is
66	only bundled inside Terminal.app on this Mac, so the mono is Menlo, the treatment's fallback.
67	
68	## The edit (`edit.js`)
69	
70	A **window** is what the engine draws: one plate per window, windows in song order. A
71	**figure** is what the caption numbers. They are not the same thing:
72	
73	| section | windows (plate) | figures |
74	|---|---|---|
75	| intro | `title` (from 0 s) | none |
76	| verse | one per lab, consecutive lines of the same lab (plate = lab key) | one per lab |
77	| chorus | `chorus` | the bench (lines before the first "Will it replicate?") and the replication |
78	| bridge | `bridge` | one per myth line (`MYTHS` in labs.js); "Now the part you skip" and "So when you ask me" have none |
79	| break | `blank` | none |
80	| drop | `drop` | one (brain growth) |
81	| final | `final` | the bench again and the grid |
82	| outro | `outro`, then the loop tail `title` (`loop: true`) | none |
83	
84	Windows open 0.2 s before their first word (no beat snap: verse motion follows the words).
85	Figures are numbered in song order over the lines present in the take; a lab whose lines are
86	all absent has no window and the numbers after it close up. On runA there are 29 figures
87	(Lampl is Fig. 7, Hamlin Fig. 19, the bridge's helper Fig. 25, the final grid Fig. 29); on the
88	radio sim 26. Section plates are registered in `SECTION_PLATES` and reported by render.mjs:
89	
90	```
91	[render] section plate ok: intro title -> title 0:00.00-0:14.73 (14.7s)
92	[render] section plate ok: chorus bench -> chorus 0:44.90-1:04.98 (20.1s), chorus 2:17.77-2:28.15 (10.4s)
93	[render] section plate ok: bridge -> bridge 2:58.12-3:40.06 (41.9s)
94	[render] section plate ok: break -> blank 3:40.06-3:41.89 (1.8s)
95	[render] section plate ok: drop -> drop 3:41.89-3:59.07 (17.2s)
96	[render] section plate ok: final grid -> final 3:59.07-4:20.26 (21.2s)
97	[render] section plate ok: outro page -> outro 4:20.26-4:28.72 (8.5s)
98	[render] section plate ok: outro loop -> title 4:28.72-4:29.60 (0.9s)
99	```
100	
101	## The loop
102	
103	The song ends on paper and the HUD (`n = 1`), and the intro begins the same way: the title,
104	the rule and the legend only arrive at "Fifteen babies". So the loop is a short tail window
105	after the last outro line has left the legend: its frames are the intro's first frames, frame
106	for frame (`story.loopTime`). The tail's shift is a whole number of beats (268.716 s on runA),
107	so the intro's beat-locked heartbeat trace restarts on the tail's beats. `--check` compares
108	tail frames with their intro frames and fails if they differ or the tail is missing.
109	
110	## The scene interface (for the second pass)
111	
112	Each lab is one module, `scenes/labs/<key>.js`, where `<key>` is the lab's key in `labs.js`,
113	its plate id and its window id. It exports:
114	
115	```js
116	export default {
117	  draw(ctx, C) { /* paint the figure for time C.t inside C.area */ },
118	};
119	```
120	
121	That is all a lab needs. It is registered already: `scenes/labs/index.js` imports every lab
122	module, `labs.js` holds its rule (the patterns that send lines to it), its caption title,
123	citation and note, and a one-line brief from the treatment. **To fill a lab, edit only its own
124	file.** The rest of the spine is frozen for the second pass: `kit.js`, `plate.js`, `edit.js`,
125	`labs.js`, `story.js`, `song.js`, the overlays and the section plates. A helper only your lab
126	needs goes in your lab file (or a sibling `scenes/labs/<key>-*.js` you own). If a caption title
127	or citation must change, say so rather than editing `labs.js`, so two agents never edit one file.
128	
129	### What C provides
130	
131	C is rebuilt for every frame by `plate.js` from (song time, window). Nothing in it carries
132	state between frames. **None of its lookups throw.**
133	
134	| group | fields |
135	|---|---|
136	| time | `t` (song time, loop-mapped), `fps`, `start`, `end`, `dur`, `lt` (= t - start), `p` (lt / dur), `inP` (0..1 over the first 0.6 s, eased: the draw-in), `erasing` (true while your plate is the previous one being erased; t is then held at your window's end) |
137	| window | `win`, `plate` (= the lab key), `params`, `lab` (the labs.js entry: key, title, cite, note, brief), `seed` |
138	| figures | `fig` (the figure the caption shows now: `num`, `title`, `cite`, `key`, `start`, `end`, `lines`), `figs` (all), `num` ({ key: number }), `figNum(key)` (number or null) |
139	| lines | `lines` (this lab's lines **present in the take**, in order; each has `text`, `n` (normalized), `start`, `end`, `words`), `line` (the current one: the last that has started, else the first), `lineIndex`, `lineP` (0..1 through it), `words` (its words: `w`, `start`, `end`, `conf`, `syl?`, `backing`) |
140	| lookups | `has(text)` → bool; `lineFor(text)` → line or null; `word(text, { line, nth, backing })` → word or null; `wordTime(text, o)` → start or `o.fallback` (default **Infinity**, "never": `t >= C.wordTime('drop')` is simply false when the word is absent); `wordEnd(text, o)`; `since(text, o)` → t - wordTime; `wseg(text, dur, o)` → 0..1 over `dur` seconds from the word (0 when absent); `seg(a, b)`, `eseg(a, b, easing)`. Text matches normalized words, exact first, then contains: "thirty six" matches "thirty-six"; a multi-word text returns the first word of the run. `o.line` is an index into `lines` or a text the line contains |
141	| beat | `B` (the beat grid: `beatDur`, `snap`, `beatEnv`, ...), `beat` (B.beatPos(t)), `onBeat(time, mode = 'beat')` (snap a time to the beat: stamps hit on the beat) |
142	| drawing | `area` ({ x: 120, y: 112, w: 1680, h: 712 }: draw only inside it), `page` (1920 x 1080), `ruleY` (864), `P` (palette: `paper`, `grid`, `ink`, `ink2` (light ink), `ink3`, the baby's clay keys), `SERIF`, `MONO`; `pen(label, opts)` → a wobble pen seeded by your plate + label; `text(s, x, y, o)` (o: size, style 'italic', weight, mono, color, align, alpha, track, reveal 0..1); `stamp(label, x, y, { t0, size, rot })`; `baby(o)`; `inkBaby(o)`; `adult(o)`; `axes(pen, x0, y0, w, h, o)`; `placeholder(o)` |
143	| engine | `env`, `L` (lyrics), `story` |
144	
145	Coordinates are always the 16:9 page (1920 x 1080 design px), in every layout: the 9:16 layout
146	scales the whole page into its upper half and draws its own caption and legend. The
147	compositor owns the paper, the caption, the legend and the HUD; a lab paints its figure only.
148	It also erases the previous plate (fade and left-to-right wipe over 0.4 s) while yours draws
149	in, so draw your figure in over `C.inP` and do not clear the page.
150	
151	**The pen** (`C.pen('axes')`, or `makePen` from `kit.js`): `line(x0, y0, x1, y1, o)`,
152	`poly(points, o)`, `spline(points, o)`, `curve(p0, c1, c2, p1, o)`, `quad`, `arc(cx, cy, r, a0,
153	a1, o)`, `ellipse(cx, cy, rx, ry, o)`, `circle`, `rect(x, y, w, h, o)` (four overshooting
154	strokes), `rrect`, `arrow`, `hatch(region, { angle, gap, p })` (region = [x, y, w, h], a
155	polygon, or a path function with `o.bbox`), `dotted(points, { gap, r })`, `dashed`. Every call
156	takes `o.p` (drawn fraction), `o.k` (a stable stroke key: give one to any stroke that appears
157	partway through a figure, or every later stroke changes its wobble), `color`, `width`,
158	`wobble`, `alpha`. `pen.with({ ... })` shares the seed and stroke counter with other defaults;
159	`pen.sub(label)` is an independent pen.
160	
161	**The baby**: `C.baby({ x, y, r, ... })` draws baby Clawd (`shared/clawd/baby.js`) on the
162	page: clay colours, no phone (both nubs free: `free` and `other`, modes `down`, `wave`, `up`,
163	`hold` (to `at`), `point`, `none`), the EEG cap on (`cap: { to: [x, y] }` in r units sets
164	where the wire ribbon goes; `cap: null` takes it off). (x, y) is the face centre; the body is
165	2.5r wide; `babyStandY(groundY, r)` from kit.js places it standing. Expressions as in the 2am
166	video: `eyes` (`open`, `half`, `closed`, `squeeze`, `wide`, `focus`), `open` (lid 0..1; `blink(t,
167	seed)` from kit.js), `look`, `mouth` (`line`, `o`, `smile`, `grin`, `press`, `none`),
168	`mouthOpen`, `brows`, `tears` (pale blue, the only other colour allowed in the baby), `blush`,
169	`tilt`. `C.inkBaby(o)` draws an ink outline copy (the other data points); `ink: 0..1` fades
170	between. `C.adult(o)` draws adult Clawd as ink line art (`shared/clawd/adult.js`, `style:
171	'ink'`): `x, y` (the ground point), `u` (unit; the body is 10u x 6u), `pose` ('stand' | 'sit'),
172	`eyes`, `mouth`, `look`, `lean`, `arms`, `p` (draw-in).
173	
174	### Rules of the page
175	
176	- Only the baby is coloured. Adults, apparatus, other data points, stamps: ink.
177	- Ink lines, hatching for shade (not fills), the light ink for grid lines, error bars and leaders.
178	- Everything is drawn in time: strokes grow (`p`), numbers roll, curves plot left to right.
179	  Nothing pops in except rubber stamps, which hit on the beat (`C.stamp(..., { t0: C.onBeat(time) })`).
180	- In verses the motion follows the words, not the beat. Hero moments land on the sung word.
181	- Pure: no `Date`, no `Math.random`, no state kept between calls. Use `hash`, `noise1` and
182	  `rng(seed)` from `core/util.js`, seeded per object.
183	- Lines that share a lab share one figure that keeps developing; `C.lineIndex` and the word
184	  lookups say where in it we are.
185	
186	### Absent lines
187	
188	The radio cut drops lines (Grant, both Hirsh-Pasek lines, Lampl's "terrible" and "ninety-five
189	percent" lines, Fenton's event counts, neonatal imitation, thirty million words), and a later
190	take may drop others. The spine handles this at three levels:
191	
192	1. A lab with none of its lines in the take has no window, no figure number, and the numbers
193	   after it close up; cross-references in captions (`{helper}` in a note) follow, and a
194	   `[bracketed]` part of a note that names an absent figure is dropped.
195	2. Inside a lab, `C.lines` holds only the lines present. Test for a beat of the figure with
196	   `C.has('front page')` or `C.lineFor('Grant')`, and key motion to words with `wordTime`,
197	   whose default "never" (Infinity) quietly skips a moment whose word is absent.
198	3. A verse line no rule matches continues the previous lab of its section (`[labs] ... continue
199	   the previous lab` in the render log), or, with none before it, gets the `unmatched`
200	   placeholder (`[labs] ... 'unmatched' placeholder`).
201	
202	Test with the radio sim: `--data data-radio-sim --check`, `?data=data-radio-sim/` in the preview.
203	
204	### Checking a lab
205	
206	- Preview `?plate=<key>` (the hero frame, 75 percent into the window), then scrub; `[` `]` jump
207	  between plates.
208	- `tools/figures.mjs` prints each lab's window time, so `--stills` can hit its words.
209	- `--check` must still pass (determinism and the loop).
210	
211	## Files
212	
213	- `song.js`: the compositor (paper, wash, plates with the erase, caption, legend, HUD; the
214	  9:16 arrangement).
215	- `edit.js`: windows, figures, numbering, cross-references, the loop tail, `SECTION_PLATES`.
216	- `labs.js`: the rule table (labs, bridge myths, section figures).
217	- `story.js`: song-level curves (loop, figure at t, HUD text, heartbeat time, rule).
218	- `plate.js`: the C contract. `kit.js`: the plate kit (frozen). `page.js`: paper, palette, fonts.
219	- `legend.js`, `caption.js`, `hud.js`: the three permanent elements.
220	- `scenes/`: `title.js` (intro and loop tail), `chorus.js` (chorus and final), `bridge.js`,
221	  `blank.js`, `drop.js`, `outro.js`, `labs/` (one module per lab, all placeholders for now).
222	- `tools/`: `figures.mjs`, `radio_sim.mjs`.
223	- Shared with the 2am video: `shared/clawd/baby.js` (baby Clawd, with the opt-in EEG cap and
224	  phone-free nubs), `shared/clawd/adult.js` (adult Clawd, with the opt-in ink style),
225	  `shared/renderer/core/ink.js` (the pen). The 2am video re-exports both characters and its
226	  frames are pixel-identical to before the move.
227	
228	## Departures and gaps (spine)
229	
230	- The heartbeat trace follows the beat grid (one ECG complex per beat). audio.json has no
231	  heartbeat onsets, and this take has no heartbeat to lock to: in the cached htdemucs_ft stems
232	  (`shared/align/work/replicate-n-1-6b228558-*/stems/`) drums and bass are silent until the beat
233	  enters at about 15 s, and no stem has a periodic thump at a heart rate over 0-15 s or
234	  258-269.6 s (the 0.32 s pulses at 265-267 s are a half-beat drum fill). A take with an
235	  audible heartbeat would want its onsets in audio.json and a drop-in change in `story.js`.
236	- The title page adds a small "METHODS · A STUDY OF ONE" kicker over the title.
237	- The break hides the legend, as the treatment lists only paper, rule and HUD.
238	- The chorus has two figure numbers (bench, replication); that is what puts Lampl at Fig. 7 as
239	  in the treatment's example, but the final grid is Fig. 29, not 31.
240	- Bridge covers are simple ink sketches; the treatment's extra beats (the 1998 door, the Georgia
241	  peach, the receipt printing, the 37 hills multiplying in place) are partly there.
242	- 9:16 is functional but plain: the 16:9 page scaled into the top half, a taller caption and
243	  legend below.

// The plate kit: drawing helpers shared by every plate (section plates and lab figures).
// FROZEN for the second pass: lab agents use these and do not edit this file; a helper only
// one lab needs lives in that lab's own file. All helpers are pure (same input, same pixels).

import { clamp, lerp, ease, seg, hash, TAU } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { mix, rgba } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/theme.js';
import { font } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/text.js';
import { makePen } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/ink.js';
import { drawBaby, BABY_GEOM } from '../../../shared/clawd/baby.js';
import { adultClawd } from '../../../shared/clawd/adult.js';
import { P, SERIF, MONO } from './page.js';

export { P, SERIF, MONO, makePen, BABY_GEOM };

/** The page (16:9 design px). Plates draw in these coordinates in every layout. */
export const PAGE = { W: 1920, H: 1080 };
/** The figure area: everything a figure draws stays inside it. */
export const AREA = { x: 120, y: 112, w: 1680, h: 712 };
/** The caption rule (the legend and caption sit below it). */
export const RULE_Y = 864;

// ---- the baby ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** Baby colours on paper: k = 1 the clay baby (the one coloured thing), k = 0 an ink outline copy. */
export function babyColors(k = 1) {
  const c = (clayHex, inkHex) => (k >= 1 ? clayHex : k <= 0 ? inkHex : mix(inkHex, clayHex, k));
  return {
    skin: c(P.skin, P.paper), skinShade: c(P.skinShade, P.paper), skinLight: c(P.skinLight, P.paper),
    ink: P.ink, line: P.ink, mouth: c(P.mouth, P.ink), white: c(P.white, P.paper),
    cloth: c(P.cloth, P.paper), clothShade: c(P.clothShade, P.paper),
    blush: c(P.blush, P.paper), tear: P.tear, paci: P.ink2, paciRing: P.paper, towel: P.paper, stain: P.paper,
  };
}

/**
 * Baby Clawd on the page: no phone (both nubs free), the EEG cap on by default, clay
 * colours (o.ink = 1 draws an ink outline copy instead; between 0 and 1 the clay fills in).
 * (o.x, o.y) is the face centre, o.r the scale (the body is 2.5r wide; the feet are 1.9r
 * below the face centre, the cap top about 1.2r above it). Every drawBaby option works:
 * eyes, open, look, mouth, mouthOpen, brows, tears, blush, free, other, tilt, outfit, cap.
 */
export function drawPaperBaby(ctx, o) {
  const k = o.ink != null ? 1 - clamp(o.ink) : 1;
  const cap = o.cap === undefined ? { color: k >= 1 ? P.cap : mix(P.paper, P.cap, k), shade: k > 0 ? P.capShade : P.paper, electrode: P.ink } : o.cap;
  drawBaby(ctx, { outfit: 'naked', ...o, C: o.C || babyColors(k), phone: false, cap: cap || null });
}
/** Where the baby's feet touch the ground, for a face centre y and scale r. */
export const babyFeetY = (y, r) => y + (BABY_GEOM.body.b + BABY_GEOM.legH) * r;
/** Face centre y for a baby standing with its feet at groundY. */
export const babyStandY = (groundY, r) => groundY - (BABY_GEOM.body.b + BABY_GEOM.legH) * r;

/** A deterministic blink: 1 (open) or a short close every few seconds, seeded per baby. */
export function blink(t, seed = 0) {
  const period = 3.1 + 1.7 * hash(seed, 3);
  const ph = ((t + hash(seed, 4) * period) % period + period) % period;
  return ph < 0.13 ? Math.abs(ph - 0.065) / 0.065 : 1;
}

/** Adult Clawd in ink line art (shared/clawd/adult.js style: 'ink'). */
export function drawInkAdult(ctx, o) {
  adultClawd(ctx, { style: 'ink', ink: P.ink, paper: P.paper, eyes: 'open', ...o });
}

// ---- type -------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Set a line of type. o: size (32), style ('normal' | 'italic'), weight (400), mono, color
 * (ink), align ('left' | 'center' | 'right'), alpha, track (letter spacing, em), reveal
 * (0..1 left-to-right wipe, for type that is written in). Returns the width.
 */
export function text(ctx, s, x, y, o = {}) {
  const size = o.size ?? 32;
  ctx.save();
  ctx.font = font(o.weight ?? 400, size, o.mono ? MONO : SERIF, o.style === 'italic' ? 'italic' : '');
  if (o.track) ctx.letterSpacing = `${(o.track * size).toFixed(2)}px`;
  ctx.fillStyle = o.color ?? P.ink;
  ctx.globalAlpha *= o.alpha ?? 1;
  ctx.textAlign = o.align ?? 'left';
  ctx.textBaseline = 'alphabetic';
  const w = ctx.measureText(s).width;
  if (o.reveal != null && o.reveal < 1) {
    if (o.reveal <= 0) { ctx.restore(); return w; }
    const x0 = o.align === 'center' ? x - w / 2 : o.align === 'right' ? x - w : x;
    ctx.beginPath(); ctx.rect(x0 - 4, y - size * 1.3, (w + 8) * o.reveal, size * 1.8); ctx.clip();
  }
  ctx.fillText(s, x, y);
  ctx.restore();
  return w;
}

// ---- rubber stamps ------------------------------------------------------------------------

const stampCache = new Map();
function stampImage(label, size, color) {
  const key = `${label}|${size}|${color}`;
  let c = stampCache.get(key);
  if (c) return c;
  const sc = 2;   // supersampled so it stays crisp when rotated
  const m = document.createElement('canvas').getContext('2d');
  m.font = font(700, size * sc, MONO);
  const tw = m.measureText(label).width;
  const pad = size * 0.55 * sc, w = Math.ceil(tw + pad * 2), h = Math.ceil(size * 1.75 * sc);
  c = document.createElement('canvas');
  c.width = w + 8; c.height = h + 8;
  const g = c.getContext('2d');
  g.translate(4, 4);
  g.strokeStyle = color; g.fillStyle = color;
  g.lineWidth = size * 0.11 * sc;
  g.strokeRect(g.lineWidth / 2, g.lineWidth / 2, w - g.lineWidth, h - g.lineWidth);
  g.lineWidth = size * 0.045 * sc;
  const ins = size * 0.2 * sc;
  g.strokeRect(ins, ins, w - ins * 2, h - ins * 2);
  g.font = font(700, size * sc, MONO);
  g.textBaseline = 'middle'; g.textAlign = 'center';
  g.fillText(label, w / 2, h / 2 + size * 0.06 * sc);
  // worn rubber: knock out specks and a few scuffs
  g.globalCompositeOperation = 'destination-out';
  const n = Math.round((w * h) / (90 * sc * sc));
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    const x = hash(i, 51) * w, y = hash(i, 52) * h, r = (0.5 + 1.6 * hash(i, 53) ** 3) * sc;
    g.globalAlpha = 0.5 + 0.5 * hash(i, 54);
    g.beginPath(); g.arc(x, y, r, 0, TAU); g.fill();
  }
  g.globalAlpha = 0.35;
  for (let i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
    g.lineWidth = (1 + 2 * hash(i, 61)) * sc;
    g.beginPath(); g.moveTo(hash(i, 62) * w, hash(i, 63) * h); g.lineTo(hash(i, 64) * w, hash(i, 65) * h); g.stroke();
  }
  c.dims = { w: (w + 8) / sc, h: (h + 8) / sc };
  stampCache.set(key, c);
  return c;
}

/**
 * A rubber stamp: invisible before t0 - 0.1, falls onto the page and hits at t0 with a small
 * squash, then stays. Ink, never colour. o: t (now), t0 (the hit; put it on a beat with
 * C.onBeat), size (40), rot (radians, default a seeded small angle), alpha, color.
 */
export function stamp(ctx, label, x, y, o = {}) {
  const t = o.t ?? Infinity, t0 = o.t0 ?? -Infinity;
  if (!(t >= t0 - 0.1)) return;
  const size = o.size ?? 40, color = o.color ?? P.ink;
  const img = stampImage(label, size, color);
  const k = clamp((t - (t0 - 0.1)) / 0.1);          // falling
  const after = t - t0;
  const fall = 1 + 0.35 * (1 - ease.inQuad(k));
  const squash = after >= 0 ? 1 - 0.1 * Math.exp(-after / 0.05) * Math.cos(after * 40) : 1;
  const rot = o.rot ?? (hash(label.length * 31 + label.charCodeAt(0), 7) - 0.5) * 0.24;
  ctx.save();
  ctx.globalAlpha *= (o.alpha ?? 0.88) * (after >= 0 ? 1 : 0.35 + 0.65 * k);
  ctx.translate(x, y);
  ctx.rotate(rot);
  ctx.scale(fall * (2 - squash), fall * squash);
  ctx.drawImage(img, -img.dims.w / 2, -img.dims.h / 2, img.dims.w, img.dims.h);
  ctx.restore();
}

// ---- figure furniture ---------------------------------------------------------------------

/** Plain axes: origin (x0, y0), lengths w (right) and h (up), ticks, labels in the serif. */
export function axes(pen, ctx, x0, y0, w, h, o = {}) {
  const p = o.p ?? 1;
  pen.arrow(x0, y0, x0 + w, y0, { p, head: 12, k: 'ax-x' });
  pen.arrow(x0, y0, x0, y0 - h, { p, head: 12, k: 'ax-y' });
  if (o.xLabel) text(ctx, o.xLabel, x0 + w, y0 + 40, { size: 26, style: 'italic', align: 'right', color: P.ink2, reveal: seg(p, 0.6, 1) });
  if (o.yLabel) text(ctx, o.yLabel, x0 - 14, y0 - h + 6, { size: 26, style: 'italic', align: 'right', color: P.ink2, reveal: seg(p, 0.6, 1) });
}

/** Panel letter (a, b, c ...) in bold italic serif, as in a multi-panel figure. */
export function panelLetter(ctx, letter, x, y, o = {}) {
  text(ctx, letter, x, y, { size: o.size ?? 38, weight: 700, style: 'italic', alpha: o.alpha ?? 1 });
}

/**
 * The placeholder figure: the framed figure area with the baby in the cap standing in it,
 * curious; the panel letter of the current line, and the lab's file name in light mono.
 * Lab modules call it (C.placeholder()) until they draw their real figure.
 */
export function placeholder(ctx, C, o = {}) {
  const A = C.area;
  const pen = C.pen('placeholder-frame', { width: 2.4 });
  const inP = C.inP;
  pen.rect(A.x, A.y, A.w, A.h, { p: inP, k: 'frame' });
  // corner ticks inside the frame (a plotting area)
  const tk = 18;
  for (const [cx, cy, sx, sy] of [[A.x + 30, A.y + 30, 1, 1], [A.x + A.w - 30, A.y + 30, -1, 1], [A.x + 30, A.y + A.h - 30, 1, -1], [A.x + A.w - 30, A.y + A.h - 30, -1, -1]]) {
    pen.with({ width: 1.4, color: P.ink2 }).poly([[cx + sx * tk, cy], [cx, cy], [cx, cy + sy * tk]], { p: inP, k: `tick${cx},${cy}` });
  }
  const letter = 'abcdefgh'[C.lineIndex] || '';
  if (C.lines.length > 1) panelLetter(ctx, letter, A.x + 48, A.y + 76, { alpha: inP });
  text(ctx, `placeholder · scenes/labs/${C.plate}.js${C.lines.length > 1 ? ` · line ${C.lineIndex + 1} of ${C.lines.length}` : ''}`, A.x + A.w - 44, A.y + 64,
    { size: 26, mono: true, align: 'right', color: P.ink2, alpha: inP });
  // the baby, standing on the frame's floor, curious: looking about, bobbing on each word
  const r = o.r ?? 112;
  const ground = A.y + A.h - 46;
  pen.with({ width: 1.6, color: P.ink2 }).line(A.x + A.w * 0.32, ground + 2, A.x + A.w * 0.68, ground + 2, { p: inP, k: 'floor' });
  const lastWord = C.words.filter((w) => w.start <= C.t).at(-1);
  const bob = lastWord ? Math.exp(-Math.max(0, C.t - lastWord.start) / 0.16) * Math.min(1, (C.t - lastWord.start) / 0.03 + 0.2) : 0;
  const lookX = Math.sin(C.t * 0.9 + C.seed * 0.01) * 0.6, lookY = -0.3 + 0.25 * Math.sin(C.t * 0.53 + 1.3);
  const x = A.x + A.w / 2, y = babyStandY(ground, r) - 7 * bob;
  drawPaperBaby(ctx, {
    x, y, r, tilt: 0.05 * Math.sin(C.t * 0.7 + C.seed * 0.02),
    eyes: 'open', open: blink(C.t, C.seed), look: [lookX, lookY], mouth: 'o', mouthOpen: 0.22 + 0.25 * bob,
    free: { mode: 'up', angle: C.t * 1.3 }, other: { mode: 'down' },
    cap: o.cap, ...o.baby,
  });
}

# Treatment: Replicate (n = 1)

A music video typeset like the methods section of a paper. Every lyric line is a figure.
The baby is the only thing on the page that is in color, and in every figure the baby is
the data point. That is the whole visual thesis: "I'm the subject. I'm the sample. I'm the
n of one."

Primary layout 16:9 (1920x1080). A paper figure is landscape. A 9:16 cut can come later
by stacking the figure over the caption.

## The page

- **Paper.** Cream lab-notebook stock (`#f4efe3`) with a faint 5 mm grid (`#e6dfcf`), a
  subtle paper grain (deterministic noise), slightly darker toward the edges. The page
  never changes; figures are drawn onto it and erased from it.
- **Ink.** One near-black (`#1d1a17`) for everything drawn: axes, apparatus, adults,
  arrows, annotations. Strokes have a slight hand wobble (deterministic noise seeded by
  the plate and the stroke index, never by wall time), as if inked with a technical pen.
  Hatching rather than fills for shade. A second, lighter ink (`#8a8378`) for grid-lines
  inside figures, error bars, dotted leaders.
- **Color.** Only the baby: baby Clawd from the 2am video (`songs/2am-crying/video/baby.js`,
  the clay orange `#D77757` family), now wearing a white EEG cap with ink electrodes and a
  ribbon of wires trailing off-frame ("the one in the little cap"). The cap is an opt-in
  option; the 2am video must stay pixel-identical. Where a figure has many data points,
  the other points are ink; the baby is the orange one. Where the lyric says "you", the
  adults (scientists, parents) are ink line drawings of adult Clawd, never colored.
- **Type.** Figure captions in a serif (system "Iowan Old Style", fallback Georgia),
  italic for the caption label: *Fig. 3.* Numbers and the HUD in a monospace ("SF Mono",
  fallback Menlo). The lyric itself is set in the serif as the figure legend, see below.

## Three permanent elements

1. **The HUD, top-left:** `n = 1` in mono, small, with a thin ink box. It holds `n = 1`
   for the whole song except the bridge, where it shows the real sample size of each myth
   as that myth is named (`n = 4`, `n = 36 (students)`, `n = 0 words`, `n = 567 · 37 labs`),
   then on the drop rolls through digits and settles back to `n = 1`, which it keeps to
   the end. The final frame of the song is just the HUD on paper.
2. **The figure caption, bottom:** a rule, then `Fig. k.` in italic serif plus a short
   title and the citation in lighter ink, e.g. *Fig. 7. Growth saltation in one infant
   (Lampl, 1992).* It changes with every figure. The figure numbers run in order across
   the song, which is a quiet joke by the final chorus (Fig. 31).
3. **The lyric legend:** the current lyric line set in the serif under the caption rule,
   two lines maximum, left-aligned, in lighter ink; each word turns full ink as it is sung
   (word-synced from `lyrics.json`). Dense lines must be readable: minimum 34 px at 1080p,
   wrap at the clause. Parentheticals in the lyric (backing vocals) are set in italic and
   smaller. This replaces the lowercase pop captions of the 2am video entirely.

## Figures, in song order

Each lyric line maps to a figure by text rule (as `clips.js` does in the 2am song). Lines
that share a lab share one figure that keeps developing (the slope keeps rising, the
scatter keeps filling). Hero moments land on the sung word.

**Intro.** The paper title block: `Replicate (n = 1)` as a paper title, then the author
line "Fifteen babies · thirty-six world-renowned scientists · one year", then an ECG/fetal
heartbeat trace drawing itself along the bottom in time with the heartbeat in the audio.
On "And me": the baby in the cap pokes in from the bottom-right corner and waves one nub.
This same page is the outro, so the song can loop (see Loop).

**Verse 1 (Love).**
- *Oxytocin.* Two curves rising on one chart, labeled "mothers" and "fathers", drawn in
  step; on "identical in dads" the second curve snaps exactly onto the first. A cotton swab
  icon at the axis origin. Caption cites Gordon et al. 2010.
- *Still face.* A three-panel strip like a figure with panels a, b, c. (a) adult Clawd in
  ink leaning toward the baby; (b) on "froze a mother's face" the adult's face goes flat
  and a 2:00 timer starts under the panel; the baby cycles through the bids as they are
  sung: smile, point ("bid"), fuss, cry (tears are the only non-orange color in the baby,
  pale blue as in the 2am video), hands to mouth; (c) on "Then she comes back" the adult
  leans back in and the baby's face settles. "We repair" is the caption title.

**Chorus (every time).** The apparatus line: a long bench drawn in ink with the stations
named in the lyric left to right, scanner ring, slope, EEG cap on a stand, swab, a ruler
marked 7 and 10 (months). The baby rides a small cart along the bench, passing through each
station on its word ("scanner" the ring, "slope" the ramp, "cap" the cap drops on, "swab"
a swab taps the cheek, "seven, then at ten" the ruler ticks). On "Will it replicate?" the
baby splits like a cell: one becomes two, two become four, each copy an ink outline, in a
dividing-cell diagram, with the original staying orange. The chorus ends with a 4x4 grid of
ink babies and one orange. In the **final chorus**, on each "(I did.)" a row of the grid
turns orange, and by "Ask me in a year" all sixteen are orange. That is the payoff; nothing
else in the song is colored, so it should feel like the page lighting up.

**Verse 2 (Crawling).**
- *Barbu-Roth.* The baby in a harness suspended over a floor of streaming dots (optic flow
  moving toward the camera, perspective grid), legs swimming on "I swim"; a small inset
  of a tumbling astronaut drawn in ink on "astronaut". On "skateboard" the harness lifts
  away and the baby is belly-down on a skateboard, crossing a mattress drawn in plan view;
  tally marks appear on "Twice. Three times."; on "a rest" the baby stops and the eyes
  close for a beat.
- *Lampl.* A growth chart, length vs days, with the classic smooth curve in light ink and
  the real data as a staircase: flat, flat, flat, a step. The notebook row
  `59.8 / 59.8 / 59.8 / 60.8` typed in mono above the step. On "two and a half centimeters
  overnight" a step rises; "sixty-three days of flat" draws a long flat run with a
  bracket. If the take contains the "front page" line, a newspaper masthead stamps in and
  a speech bubble from an ink figure is struck through. The baby stands on the current
  step of the staircase, growing by a few pixels with each step.
- *Adolph.* The adjustable slope: a ramp on a hinge with a protractor arc, the angle
  readout in mono. Each "six degrees" raises it 6° with a ratchet; by "sheer drop" it is
  near vertical. The baby is at the top, peering over. If the Grant line is present, an ink
  toddler approaches at the shallow pace and goes straight off the edge in a little arc
  (no impact drawn, just a dotted trajectory and a "*" footnote). Step counter and fall
  counter in the corner tick up to `2,368 /h` and `17`. On "Crawling isn't mandatory" a row
  of small ink icons for bum-shuffle, bear-crawl, crawl, with the baby doing the
  bum-shuffle, happily.

**Verse 3 (Words, Sleep).**
- *Hirsh-Pasek* (if present). A speech waveform of a Cinderella sentence, drawn as an ink
  envelope, with pauses inserted: ones at clause boundaries marked with a tick, one in the
  middle of a clause marked with a cross. Two loudspeaker icons left and right; the baby's
  head turns to the natural one.
- *Saffran.* A stream of syllables `pa bee coo go la too ...` scrolling by in mono, with
  arcs above showing transitional probabilities: `1.0` arcs inside words, `0.33` between.
  A counter of repetitions ticks to 45 over two minutes on the HUD line. On "I'm carving
  speech" the word boundaries cut in as vertical lines and the words stack.
- *Jarvis.* A phylogenetic tree in ink. On "three birds, five mammals" the vocal-learner
  branches thicken and get their labels in order as they are sung: parrots, bats, elephants,
  seals, dolphins, songbirds, hummingbirds; on "This girl" the last branch ends in the baby,
  orange, holding a tiny microphone. A small dancing silhouette footnote for "danced six
  hours a day".
- *Fenton.* A scatter: x is time of day (0 to 24 h), y is age in months (0 to 12). Sleep
  events as dots. At one month the dots are noise; as "a hint by eight" and "a rhythm by a
  year" are sung, bands form (night sleep, two naps). Big counters in mono roll: 1,000
  babies, 6,000,000 events. On "(He hasn't published it.)" a rubber stamp hits the figure:
  `UNPUBLISHED`, at a slight angle. The caption reads "(Fenton & Wallisch, n.d.)".
- *Blumberg.* The baby asleep on its side in the cap. On "twitch" one leg jerks and a sonar
  ring expands from it; a body map beside it (a schematic figure, ink) lights the matching
  limb; "a thousand neurons" shows as a burst of dots along the map.
- *Spencer* (if present). Two small boxes labeled hippocampus and cortex; sleep spindles
  drawn as waveform bursts carry index cards from one box to the other. Two naps and a
  night as three cards.

**Verse 4 (First Steps, Part Two).**
- *Dominici* (if present). Two EMG traces, then four; a row of ink animals walking:
  rat, cat, macaque, guineafowl, then the baby.
- *Cowgill.* A suitcase labeled `130 lb` dragged on a dotted path across a map of fifteen
  tiny museum icons (columns and a pediment), a counter to 600 at the side. On "Reviewer 2"
  a margin note in a different hand appears: "children's bones don't respond to exercise",
  and on "Take that back" a femur cross-section morphs from a wide oval to a teardrop and
  the note is struck through.
- *Walle* (if present). A word counter like a lab LENA device; two babies, one crawling,
  one walking, identical word count heard, different word count known.
- *Hespos.* A block hanging in midair with no support, a question mark drawn and erased;
  looking-time bars: the "possible" bar short, the "impossible" bar twice as long, growing
  as the baby stares.
- *Kang Lee.* Two monkey faces drawn in ink at six months, clearly different; on "Nine
  months" they blur toward one another until identical; the adult Clawd in the margin
  shrugs ("like you, not at all").
- *Hamlin.* A hill in profile; a round puppet climbs, a square puppet shoves it down, a
  triangle pushes it up; the baby's nub reaches for the helper. Caption cites Hamlin et al.
  2007 and adds "(but see Fig. 27)", pointing at the bridge.
- *Warneken.* An ink adult "drops" a cup, dotted fall; a stopwatch counts to 10 s as the
  baby picks it up and hands it back. On "Pay me with a toy" a toy appears; on "I'll stop"
  the baby sits down and folds its nubs. "Also the human part" is the caption title.

**Bridge (the part you skip).** The page turns dim (ink wash at 15 percent), the figures are
journal covers and products drawn as ink line art, one per myth, each stamped. The HUD takes
the sample size as each lands:
- The Wonder Weeks: a calendar with ten leaps circled; stamp `n = 4`; a second stamp
  `1998` over a small figure leaving a door.
- Mozart effect: a CD; stamp `n = 36 (college students)`; a stack of CDs with a Georgia
  peach outline; the HUD shows `$105,000` for a beat.
- Baby Einstein: a DVD case; stamp `0 new words`; a receipt prints out: `REFUND $15.99`.
- Neonatal imitation (if present): a tongue-out photo as an ink sketch; `n = 6 (1977)` and
  under it `n = 106 (2016): nothing`.
- The helper and the hinderer: the puppet hill from Fig. 20 returns, small, under a
  journal masthead `Nature, 2007`; on "ManyBabies four" the figure multiplies into
  thirty-seven tiny hills; the number `49.3 %` and a coin, drawn, flipping in the air.
- Thirty million words (if present): a counter at 30,000,000 with `n = 42 · 1 h / month`
  stamped across it.
- "So when you ask me... will it replicate?": everything on the page fades to lighter ink
  except the HUD, which shows the myth sample sizes in a fast shuffle.

**Break ("Here's what did.").** The page goes blank. Only the paper, the caption rule, the
HUD at `n = ?`. One beat of nothing.

**Drop.** A single ink cell in the center. It divides on the beat: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, the
outlines packing into the shape of a brain in profile; a volume counter in mono rolls to
`+101 %`; `2,500 synapses / neuron` ticks up and then is visibly pruned (lines erased);
`weight x3` and `length x1.5` appear as a small table. The baby appears in the center of the
brain, orange, on "Me." On "I'm the replication" the HUD settles on `n = 1`.

**Final chorus.** The apparatus bench again, but the grid payoff described above: the
copies turn orange row by row on "(I did.)".

**Outro.** Back to the title page. The heartbeat trace continues from where the intro left
it. "Hardest problem in the universe. We should let them sleep." is set as a pull quote in
the serif, attributed in lighter ink. On "n equals one" the title block fades and leaves the
HUD alone on the paper: `n = 1`. The baby is asleep in the corner in the cap.

## Loop

Like the 2am video: the outro frame is the intro frame, so the file loops. The loop anchor is
the heartbeat trace and the title block, which are identical at the start and the end; the
`--check` loop test in `render.mjs` must pass.

## Motion rules

- Everything is drawn in time: strokes grow, numbers roll, curves plot left to right.
  Nothing pops in except rubber stamps, which hit on the beat with a small squash.
- Beat-synced motion is allowed in the choruses (the cart, the cell division) and the drop.
  In verses the motion follows the words, not the beat; this is a documentary, not a club.
- The baby's expression follows the lyric: curious in the labs, unimpressed in the bridge,
  asleep in the outro. Reuse the 2am baby's expression parameters.
- Deterministic: every frame is a pure function of song time, as in the 2am renderer.

## Data

- Sheet: `songs/baby-facts/suno_lyrics_v1_short.txt` (the keeper's sheet). Sections:
  intro, verse1, chorus1, verse2, verse3, chorus2, verse4, bridge, break, drop, final, outro.
- Keeper for development: `songs/baby-facts/audio/runA/replicate-n-1-6b228558.mp3`
  (4:29, 95.6 BPM), aligned in `songs/baby-facts/audio/runA/data/`.
- A radio cut (`suno_lyrics_v2_radio.txt`, seven lines shorter) is being generated in runs
  C and C2; if a take is good it replaces the keeper, so figures keyed to lines that the
  radio cut dropped (Grant, Hirsh-Pasek, the "ninety-five percent" line, Fenton's event
  counts, neonatal imitation, thirty million words) must degrade gracefully when the line
  is absent.

# Replicate (n = 1)

Dense fact-rap from the point of view of the baby being studied. The spine is Netflix's
*Babies* (2020): the title card ("15 babies, filmed over one year, 36 world-renowned
scientists") is the chorus, each verse is a tour of the labs in episode order, and the
bridge is the replication crisis. The hook "Will it replicate?" means three things: cells
dividing, the study being reproduced, and the baby daring the scientists. The turn at the
end: the only result that always replicates is the baby.

Every fact is on `FACTS.md` with a source. Verses are 12 lines at ~96 BPM, one line per bar,
musical-theatre hip-hop patter. "this girl" in verse 3 is the narrator; swap for "this kid".

---

**[Intro]** *(spoken, documentary narrator, over a fetal heartbeat; then the beat)*
> Fifteen babies. Filmed over one year. Thirty-six world-renowned scientists.
> And me. I'm the one in the little cap.

**[Verse 1: Love]**
> Day one: skin to skin, and the oxytocin's climbing.
> Feldman swabs the spit of eighty couples, and the timing's
> identical in dads. "Fatherhood is biological."
> Forty-eight gay fathers, amygdala lit: that's data, not anecdotal.
> Tronick, '68: "This emotion stuff is not what you want to do."
> So he froze a mother's face for two minutes and watched me come unglued:
> I smile, I bid, I fuss, I cry, I chew my hands to cope.
> Then she comes back. We repair. That's the study. That's the hope.

**[Chorus]**
> I'm the subject. I'm the sample. I'm the n of one.
> Put me in the scanner, put me on the slope. I'm not done.
> Cap on my head, swab in my cheek, measure me at seven, then at ten.
> Will it replicate? *(Will it replicate?)*
> Will it replicate? *(Will it replicate?)*
> Fifteen babies. Thirty-six scientists. One year.
> Will it replicate? Ask me in a year.

**[Verse 2: Crawling]**
> Barbu-Roth did nuclear physics; now she's hanging me in air.
> Dots stream under me. I swim. "A newborn's an astronaut," I swear.
> "We're still quadrupeds inside." She strapped a skateboard to my chest:
> two days old, I crossed the mattress. Twice. Three times. Then I had a rest.
> Lampl's neighbor's kid: sixty-two point seven, then sixty-four point five.
> "I'm terrible at this," she thought. Nope. That's just how we arrive:
> up to two and a half centimeters overnight, then sixty-three days of flat.
> Ninety-five percent of babyhood is growth-free. Front page. A big name mocked her for that.
> Adolph raised the slope six degrees a go, up to a sheer drop.
> Grant approached it like the shallow ones and went straight off the top.
> Two thousand three hundred sixty-eight steps an hour, seventeen falls, no stall.
> "Crawling isn't mandatory." Bum-shuffle, bear-crawl, whatever gets you down the hall.

**[Verse 3: Words, Sleep]**
> Hirsh-Pasek splits up Cinderella, pauses in the middle of a clause.
> I turn to where it breathes right. Can't talk yet. I know the laws.
> Saffran: pabeecoo, golatoo, two minutes, forty-five reps each.
> Probability one inside a word, point three across: I'm carving speech.
> Jarvis danced six hours a day, then counted who can learn a sound in the world:
> three birds, five mammals. Parrots, bats, elephants, seals, dolphins, songbirds, hummingbirds. This girl.
> Fenton's app: a thousand babies, six million events, logged to the minute:
> one-point-five million sleeps, two-point-five million feeds, the diapers, a million and a half in it.
> Random at one month, a hint by eight, a rhythm by a year. *(He hasn't published it.)*
> Blumberg says my twitch is sonar: one ping, a thousand neurons, mapping where my limbs fit.
> Spencer: hippocampus small, so I file in bouts; the spindles move the day
> from hippocampus to cortex. Two naps and a night. That's not lazy. That's the way.

**[Chorus]**

**[Verse 4: First Steps, then Part Two]** *(double-time on the Part Two lines)*
> Dominici wires my legs at two days old: two muscle patterns, and I step;
> toddlers add two more. Rats, cats, macaques, guineafowl: same ancient rep.
> Cowgill hauled a hundred thirty pounds through fifteen museums, six hundred kids' bones in the stack.
> Reviewer 2 said "children's bones don't respond to exercise." Toddler femurs are wide ovals. Take that back.
> Walle: walkers know more words than crawlers, and they hear the exact same amount.
> It's you: "Du—" "Duck." "Duck!" Three hundred seventy-four words in five minutes. I count.
> Part Two: Hespos hangs a block in midair; I stare twice as long. I know how things fall.
> Kang Lee: six months, I tell two monkeys apart. Nine months, like you, not at all.
> Saxe scans her own son, awake: face patch, place patch, same spots as yours.
> Hamlin's puppet climbs; I reach for the helper. *(Hold that thought. It's back in the bridge, of course.)*
> Carpenter: twelve months, I point to show, not to get. You look. That's the human part.
> Warneken "drops" a cup; eighteen months, I hand it back in ten seconds. Pay me with a toy and I'll stop. Also the human part.

**[Bridge: the part you skip]** *(half-time, sparse, the baby as Reviewer 2)*
> Now the part you skip.
> The Wonder Weeks: ten leaps on a calendar. Tested on four babies by a grad student. Nothing. The man behind it lost his job in '98.
> Mozart effect: thirty-six college students, ten minutes, never once a baby. Georgia spent a hundred and five grand on CDs for newborns.
> Baby Einstein: a month of DVDs, zero new words. Disney refunded fifteen ninety-nine a disc.
> Neonatal imitation: six babies in '77 stuck their tongues out. A hundred and six in 2016 did nothing.
> The helper and the hinderer: *Nature*, 2007. ManyBabies 4: five hundred sixty-seven babies, thirty-seven labs. Forty-nine point three percent. A coin.
> Thirty million words: forty-two families, one hour a month.
> So when you ask me... will it replicate?
> *(beat drops out)*
> Here's what did.
> Me. Brain volume up a hundred and one percent in year one. Twenty-five hundred synapses a neuron, then more, then pruned. Weight tripled. Length up half.
> I'm the replication. I'm the only result that always comes in.

**[Final Chorus]**
> I'm the subject. I'm the sample. I'm the n of one.
> Put me in the scanner, put me on the slope. I'm not done.
> Cap on my head, swab in my cheek, measure me at seven, then at ten.
> Will it replicate? *(I did.)*
> Will it replicate? *(I did.)*
> Fifteen babies. Thirty-six scientists. One year.
> Will it replicate? Ask me in a year.

**[Outro]** *(spoken; the beat stops; heartbeat)*
> "Hardest problem in the universe. We should let them sleep."
> n equals one.

---

| Saxe scans her son | Deen 2017; Ep 7 |

| pointing to show at 12 months | Carpenter 1998; Liszkowski 2004 |

| "hardest problem in the universe" | Fenton's closer, Ep 5 |

## Cuts for a shorter edit (~2:45)

Drop verse 4's First Steps half (keep Part Two), and cut the bridge to Wonder Weeks, Mozart,
ManyBabies, then the turn.

// The rule table: lyric text -> lab figure (as clips.js maps clauses to clip types in the
// 2am song). Patterns test the normalized line text (core/lyrics.js norm(): lowercase,
// punctuation and apostrophes dropped, hyphens become spaces, so "Hirsh-Pasek" is
// "hirsh pasek"). First match wins; only lines in a verse section are tested. A verse line
// that matches nothing continues the previous lab of its section (logged), else it gets the
// 'unmatched' placeholder (logged loudly).
//
// Every lab of all three sheets is listed (the keeper's suno_lyrics_v1_short.txt, the radio
// cut suno_lyrics_v2_radio.txt and the full LYRICS.md): a lab whose lines are all absent from
// the take simply has no window, and the figure numbers after it close up.
//
// Fields: key (= plate id = scenes/labs/<key>.js), title and cite (the caption: "Fig. k.
// <title> (<cite>)"), note (an extra parenthetical after the citation; {key} is the number
// of another figure, and a [bracketed] part is dropped when a figure it names is absent),
// match (patterns), brief (what the real figure shows, from TREATMENT.md).

/** Section figures that are not labs. */
export const SECTION_FIGS = {
  bench: { title: 'Experimental apparatus', cite: 'this study' },
  replication: { title: 'Replication by division', cite: 'n = 1' },
  benchFinal: { title: 'Experimental apparatus, again', cite: 'this study' },
  replicationFinal: { title: 'Sixteen replications of one result', cite: 'this study' },
  brain: { title: 'Brain growth in year one', cite: 'Knickmeyer et al., 2008; Huttenlocher, 1997' },
};

export const LAB = Object.fromEntries(LABS.map((l) => [l.key, l]));
export const MYTH = Object.fromEntries(MYTHS.map((m) => [m.key, m]));

/** The lab a verse line belongs to by its own text (null: no rule matched). */
export function labOf(line) {
  for (const lab of LABS) if (lab.match.some((re) => re.test(line.n))) return lab.key;
  return null;
}

/** The bridge myth a line names (null: not a myth line, e.g. "Now the part you skip"). */
export function mythOf(line) {
  for (const m of MYTHS) if (m.match.some((re) => re.test(line.n))) return m.key;
  return null;
}

/** Chorus lines from the first "Will it replicate?" on are the replication figure. */
export const isReplicateLine = (line) => /^will it replicate/.test(line.n);

input
description Read plate.js and print figure tables
cat /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/songs/baby-facts/video/plate.js; node /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/songs/baby-facts/video/tools/figures.mjs; node /Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop/songs/baby-facts/video/tools/figures.mjs --data data-radio-sim
result
// The plate context C: everything a plate's draw(ctx, C) may read. Built fresh for every
// frame from (song time, window); nothing in it carries state between frames. The lookups
// never throw: a word or line the take does not have comes back null (word, lineFor), false
// (has), a fallback (wordTime: Infinity, i.e. "never"), or 0 (wseg).
//
//   time      t (song time, loop-mapped), fps, start, end, dur, lt (t - start), p (lt / dur),
//             inP (0..1 draw-in over the first 0.6 s), erasing (true while this plate is the
//             previous one being erased; t is then held at its end)
//   window    win, plate (= lab key for labs), params, lab (the labs.js entry), seed
//   figures   fig (the figure the caption shows now, or null), figs (all), num ({ key: n }),
//             figNum(key) -> number | null
//   lines     lines (this window's lines in the take), line (the current one: the last that
//             has started, else the first), lineIndex, lineP (0..1 through it), words (its words)
//   lookups   has(text), lineFor(text), word(text, { line, nth, backing }), wordTime(text, o),
//             wordEnd(text, o), since(text, o), wseg(text, dur, o), seg(a, b), eseg(a, b, fn)
//             text matches normalized words ("thirty six" matches "thirty-six"); o.line is an
//             index into lines or a text match; o.fallback replaces "absent"
//   beat      B (beat grid), beat (B.beatPos(t)), onBeat(time, mode = 'beat') -> snapped time
//   drawing   area (the figure area), page, ruleY, P (palette), SERIF, MONO,
//             pen(label, opts) a wobble pen seeded by plate + label, text(s, x, y, o),
//             stamp(label, x, y, o) (t defaults to C.t), baby(o), inkBaby(o), adult(o),
//             axes(pen, x0, y0, w, h, o), placeholder(o)
//   engine    env, L (lyrics), story

import { clamp, seg, eseg, ease, strSeed } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { norm } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/lyrics.js';
import { makePen } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/ink.js';
import { LAB } from './labs.js';
import { LEGEND_LEAD } from './edit.js';
import { P, SERIF, MONO, PAGE, AREA, RULE_Y, text, stamp, axes, placeholder, drawPaperBaby, drawInkAdult } from './kit.js';

const matches = (w, m) => { const nw = norm(w.w); return nw === m || nw.includes(m); };

export function makePlateContext(ctx, env, t, w, { erasing = false } = {}) {
  const st = env.story, B = env.B, L = env.L;
  const lines = w.lines || [];
  let li = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) if (lines[i].start - LEGEND_LEAD <= t) li = i;
  const line = lines[li] || null;
  const seed = strSeed(w.plate + ':' + (w.params.section || w.params.lab || ''));
  const pool = (o) => (o.line == null ? lines : typeof o.line === 'number' ? [lines[o.line]].filter(Boolean)
    : lines.filter((l) => l.n.includes(norm(o.line))));
  const C = {
    t, fps: env.fps, start: w.start, end: w.end, dur: w.dur, lt: t - w.start, p: clamp((t - w.start) / w.dur),
    inP: ease.outCubic(seg(t, w.start, w.start + 0.6)), erasing,
    win: w, plate: w.plate, params: w.params, lab: LAB[w.plate] || null, seed,
    fig: st.figureAt(t), figs: st.figs, num: st.num, figNum: (k) => st.num[k] ?? null,
    lines, line, lineIndex: li, lineP: line ? seg(t, line.start, line.end) : 0, words: line ? line.words : [],
    has: (m) => lines.some((l) => l.n.includes(norm(m))),
    lineFor: (m) => lines.find((l) => l.n.includes(norm(m))) || null,
    word(m, o = {}) {
      const mm = norm(m);
      const ok = (wd) => o.backing == null || !!wd.backing === !!o.backing;
      // exact words first ("six" is not "sixty"), then words that contain the text
      for (const test of [(wd) => norm(wd.w) === mm, (wd) => matches(wd, mm)]) {
        let n = o.nth ?? 0;
        for (const l of pool(o)) for (const wd of l.words) if (ok(wd) && test(wd) && n-- === 0) return wd;
      }
      // a multi-word match ("thirty six" is one word, "one year" two): first word of the run
      const parts = mm.split(' ');
      if (parts.length > 1) for (const l of pool(o)) for (let k = 0; k + parts.length <= l.words.length; k++) {
        if (parts.every((p, j) => norm(l.words[k + j].w) === p)) return l.words[k];
      }
      return null;
    },
    wordTime(m, o = {}) { const wd = C.word(m, o); return wd ? wd.start : (o.fallback ?? Infinity); },
    wordEnd(m, o = {}) { const wd = C.word(m, o); return wd ? wd.end : (o.fallback ?? Infinity); },
    since(m, o = {}) { return t - C.wordTime(m, o); },
    wseg(m, dur = 0.5, o = {}) { const t0 = C.wordTime(m, o); return isFinite(t0) ? clamp((t - t0) / dur) : 0; },
    seg: (a, b) => seg(t, a, b), eseg: (a, b, fn) => eseg(t, a, b, fn),
    B, beat: B.beatPos(t), onBeat: (time, mode = 'beat') => (isFinite(time) ? B.snap(time, mode) : time),
    area: AREA, page: PAGE, ruleY: RULE_Y, P, SERIF, MONO,
    pen: (label = 'main', opts = {}) => makePen(ctx, `${w.plate}/${label}`, { color: P.ink, ...opts }),
    text: (s, x, y, o) => text(ctx, s, x, y, o),
    stamp: (label, x, y, o = {}) => stamp(ctx, label, x, y, { t, ...o }),
    baby: (o) => drawPaperBaby(ctx, o),
    inkBaby: (o) => drawPaperBaby(ctx, { ...o, ink: o.ink ?? 1 }),
    adult: (o) => drawInkAdult(ctx, { pen: makePen(ctx, `${w.plate}/adult${o.k ?? ''}`, { color: P.ink }), ...o }),
    axes: (pen, x0, y0, w0, h0, o) => axes(pen, ctx, x0, y0, w0, h0, o),
    placeholder: (o) => placeholder(ctx, C, o),
    env, L, story: st,
  };
  return C;
}

#!/usr/bin/env node
// Print the edit for a timing pair: windows (plates) and the numbered figures with their
// captions, as the renderer will build them. No browser needed.
//   node songs/baby-facts/video/tools/figures.mjs [--data data-radio-sim]
import fs from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { makeBeats } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/beats.js';
import { makeLyrics } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/lyrics.js';
import { fmtTime } from '../../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { makePlan } from '../edit.js';
import { captionRuns } from '../caption.js';

const here = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
const argv = process.argv.slice(2);
const dir = path.join(here, '..', argv.includes('--data') ? argv[argv.indexOf('--data') + 1] : 'data');
const audio = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, 'audio.json'), 'utf8'));
const lyrics = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, 'lyrics.json'), 'utf8'));
const B = makeBeats(audio);
const L = makeLyrics(lyrics, B);
const plan = makePlan(L, B);
console.log(`windows (${plan.entries.length}):`);
for (const e of plan.entries) console.log(`  ${fmtTime(e.at.t ?? e.at.time ?? 0).padStart(8)}  ${e.plate.padEnd(12)} ${e.fromSection ? `[${e.fromSection}]` : ''}  ${(e.params.lines || []).length} line(s)`);
console.log(`figures (${plan.figs.length}):`);
for (const f of plan.figs) console.log(`  ${fmtTime(f.start).padStart(8)}  ${f.key.padEnd(20)} ${captionRuns(f).map((r) => r.text).join('')}`);
if (plan.loop) console.log(`loop tail at ${fmtTime(plan.loop.start)} (shift ${plan.loop.shift.toFixed(3)} s)`);

import fs from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
const root = '/Users/jalex/projects/clawdpop';
const { makeBeats } = await import(root + '/shared/renderer/core/beats.js');
const { makeLyrics } = await import(root + '/shared/renderer/core/lyrics.js');
const { makePlan } = await import(root + '/songs/baby-facts/video/edit.js');
const argv = process.argv.slice(2);
const dir = path.join(root, 'songs/baby-facts/video', argv[0] || 'data');
const want = (argv[1] || 'feldman,abraham,tronick,barburoth,lampl,adolph,hirshpasek,saffran').split(',');
const audio = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, 'audio.json'), 'utf8'));
const lyrics = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, 'lyrics.json'), 'utf8'));
const B = makeBeats(audio);
const L = makeLyrics(lyrics, B);
const plan = makePlan(L, B);
const ents = plan.entries;
for (let i = 0; i < ents.length; i++) {
  const e = ents[i];
  if (!want.includes(e.plate)) continue;
  const end = ents[i + 1] ? (ents[i + 1].at.t ?? ents[i + 1].at.time) : null;
  console.log(`== ${e.plate} ${(e.at.t ?? e.at.time).toFixed(2)} - ${end?.toFixed(2)}  keys=${Object.keys(e.params)}`);
  for (const l of e.params.lines || []) {
    console.log(`  [${l.start.toFixed(2)}-${l.end.toFixed(2)}] ${l.text}`);
    console.log('     ' + l.words.map((w) => `${w.w}@${w.start.toFixed(2)}`).join(' '));
  }
}
console.log('beatDur', B.beatDur);

// The page: cream lab-notebook stock with a faint 5 mm grid, a deterministic grain and a
// slight darkening toward the edges. It never changes; figures are drawn onto it and
// erased from it. The texture is baked once per output size (a pure function of the size,
// so every worker bakes the same pixels) and blitted every frame.

import { hash, clamp } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/util.js';
import { rgba, mix } from '../../../shared/renderer/core/theme.js';

/** The palette. Only the baby is coloured; everything else is ink on paper. */
export const P = {
  paper: '#f4efe3', grid: '#e6dfcf', ink: '#1d1a17', ink2: '#8a8378', ink3: '#b9b1a3',
  // baby Clawd (the 2am clay family): the one coloured thing on the page
  skin: '#d77757', skinShade: '#b35a3d', skinLight: '#f5b394', mouth: '#4a1f2a', white: '#fff8f0',
  blush: '#f0848a', tear: '#a9d6f2', cloth: '#fbf8f1', clothShade: '#ddd6c8', cap: '#fbfaf6', capShade: '#ddd7ca',
};

export const SERIF = '"Paper Serif", "Iowan Old Style", Georgia, serif';
export const MONO = '"Paper Mono", "SF Mono", Menlo, monospace';
/** Faces declared in index.html (local() system fonts under alias families). */
export const FONTS = [
  { family: 'Paper Serif', weight: 400 }, { family: 'Paper Serif', weight: 400, style: 'italic' },
  { family: 'Paper Serif', weight: 700 }, { family: 'Paper Serif', weight: 700, style: 'italic' },
  { family: 'Paper Mono', weight: 400 }, { family: 'Paper Mono', weight: 700 },
];

/** Grid pitch in design px: 5 mm on a page about 300 mm wide. */
export const GRID_PX = 32;

const cache = new Map();

/**
 * The paper texture for an output of pw x ph pixels showing a W x H design page.
 * Grain is per output pixel (so it survives downscaling), mottling is low-frequency.
 */
export function paperTexture(pw, ph, W, H) {
  const key = `${pw}x${ph}:${W}x${H}`;
  let c = cache.get(key);
  if (c) return c;
  c = document.createElement('canvas');
  c.width = pw; c.height = ph;
  const g = c.getContext('2d');
  const sx = pw / W, sy = ph / H;
  g.fillStyle = P.paper;
  g.fillRect(0, 0, pw, ph);
  // mottling: two faint random fields, smoothly upscaled (the stock is not perfectly even)
  for (const [cols, alpha, salt] of [[24, 0.022, 9001], [96, 0.014, 9002]]) {
    const mw = cols, mh = Math.max(4, Math.round((cols * ph) / pw));
    const m = document.createElement('canvas');
    m.width = mw; m.height = mh;
    const mg = m.getContext('2d');
    const md = mg.createImageData(mw, mh);
    for (let i = 0; i < mw * mh; i++) {
      const v = hash(i, salt);
      const base = v < 0.5 ? 60 : 255;
      md.data[i * 4] = base; md.data[i * 4 + 1] = base * 0.95; md.data[i * 4 + 2] = base * 0.85;
      md.data[i * 4 + 3] = Math.round(Math.abs(v - 0.5) * 2 * 255);
    }
    mg.putImageData(md, 0, 0);
    g.save();
    g.imageSmoothingEnabled = true;
    g.imageSmoothingQuality = 'high';
    g.globalAlpha = alpha;
    g.drawImage(m, 0, 0, pw, ph);
    g.restore();
  }
  // the 5 mm grid, slightly broken up (printed on cheap stock)
  g.save();
  g.scale(sx, sy);
  g.strokeStyle = P.grid;
  g.lineWidth = 1.6;
  g.beginPath();
  const x0 = ((W / 2) % GRID_PX), y0 = ((H / 2) % GRID_PX);
  for (let x = x0; x < W; x += GRID_PX) { g.moveTo(x, 0); g.lineTo(x, H); }
  for (let y = y0; y < H; y += GRID_PX) { g.moveTo(0, y); g.lineTo(W, y); }
  g.stroke();
  g.restore();
  // grain: per-pixel noise, a little warmer in the darks
  const img = g.getImageData(0, 0, pw, ph);
  const d = img.data;
  for (let i = 0, n = pw * ph; i < n; i++) {
